[APRII-JUNE, CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWCHWANG, ISTH JULY 1901. -SIR, I.-The Imperial Russian Provisional Civil Administration having opened, on the 1ath August i0oo, a Customu House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Duties on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistics of that trade from ist April to 3oth June 19o. On all cargoes arriving or leaving by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were collected at Treaty Tariff rates, and Likin at the rate of 1% per cent. ad valorem on Imports and 14 per cent. ad valorem on Exports, with the exception of the Duties on certain specified staples, which were reduced on ist May Igo1 to the rate formerly assessed by the Native Customs. Boats trading up river plid Tonnage Dues, but no Duties were levied on their cargoes. II. W PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended June 80, 1901. *A
230 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 3864. YANGTZE RIVER-HANKOW DISTRICT. ESTABLISHMENT OF RANGE BEACONS AND LIGHTS FOR KING-KAU ROCKS. NOTICE is hereby given that range beacons and lights have been, established at Hsia-sha-hu, on theeast bank of the riv4r about 2 miles above the King-kau Rocks, to guide vessels between the rock in the centre of the river and the rocks lying abreast of it off the east bank. The rear and higher beacon exhibits a red light 48 feet above ground; the fore beacon, a white light 36 feet above ground. The line of these beacons passes about 350 feet to the eastward of the central reek and a similar distance off the broken water forming the east side of the channel. The beacon-poles are surmounted by triangular shapes and painted black. SHANGHAI, 211d Ma~y 1901. No. 365. YANGTZE RIVER-CH INKIANG DISTRICT. RANGE BEACONS AND LIGHTS ESTABLISHED AT SILVER ISLAND CROSSING. NOTICE is hereby given that range beacons and lights have been established on the left bank of the river, to the northward of Feather Island, to guide vessels over the Silver Island Crossing. The rear and higher beacon bears a square black shape, exhibits a red light, and its light-hut is painted black. The fore beacon bears a diamond black and white shape,. exhibits a white light, and its light-hut is painted white.
1901.1 ] NOTIFICATIONS. 231 A recent survey shows a least depth of 25 feet (at Chinkiang zero) on the line marked by these beacons; but on either side, in the neighbourhood of the Silver Island Spit Buoy, there are patches of 23 feet at a distance of about ioo feet from the line. CAUTION. It is uncertain to what extent the axis of the channel may shift, and the depth is liable to somewhat rapid chaige; consequently the information given above as to the amount of water should be used with caution. SHANGMA , 2d May 1901. No. 386. TIENTSIN DISTRIGT. ALTERATION IN CHARACTER OF TSAO-FEI-TIEN LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the character of the light on Tsao-fei-tien Island (marked on the Admiralty Charts as Sha-lui-tien) has been changed. The new light is dioptric, group flashing, of the fourth order, showing double .white flashes at intervals of 20 seconds. The light is elevated 5 1 feet above the level of the sea, and in clear weather it should be visible at a distance of 121 nautical miles. The height of the tower from base to lantern vane has been increased to 57 feet. SHANGHA, 27th May 1901.

APPENDIX . KOWLOON AND LAPPA QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. APRIL-JUNE 901.
25 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE (Received 25th July 191o.) CUSTOM HOUSE, * KOWLOON, 15TH JULY 1901. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the second quarter of the year 1901. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan (* I), Lintin (fit fj)) and Shamehun (A j), on the north and west; Samun (E (&#39;), on the east; and Shaiichung (&amp;amp; A j) and Shatowkok (f ] j), on the Mirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is called IL N r. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
